Swelling tests in a phosphate-buffered saline solution show that its best performing designs reached a maximum absorption after 3–5 h of 1468–2026% of its original weight \[ [121](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR121 "Zidarič T, Gradišnik L, Frangež T, Šoštarič M, Korunič E, Maver T, Maver U (2024) Novel 3D printed polysaccharide-based materials with prebiotic activity for potential treatment of diaper rash. Int J Biol Macromol 269:131958.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2024.131958                                    ")\]. Three dimensional printing of top sheet material with specific application in sanitary napkins has the potential to create an exciting new narrative and transform this area of research and designs.
  
  The incorporation of nanotechnology in top sheets of AHPs is popular due to its useful antimicrobial properties. Liu _et. al._ designed a biodegradable top sheet with ZnO nanoparticles anchored on the nonwoven cellulose with cellulose grafted tert-butyl isocyanate. The fabric changed from hydrophilic to hydrophobic (water contact angle of 139.7°), it displayed a breaking strength of 66.9 N/5cm and air permeability of 2621 mm/s. It also showed good antibacterial activity against _E. coli_ and _S. aureus_ of 94.8% and 93.2%, respectively \[ [126](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR126 "Liu M, Ma C, Chen Y, Wang Y, Xu J, Li Z, Deng L, Zou L, Wu J, Wang H (2024) Cellulose nonwoven with fast liquid-discharging and anti-return properties: a microplastic-free surface layer for disposable absorbent hygiene products. Chem Eng J 490:151291.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cej.2024.151291                                    ")\]. Another study incorporated ZnO nanoparticles in a curcumin/PCL electrospun nonwoven top sheet. The top sheet displayed overall biocompatibility, biodegradability (28% weight loss after 28 days), antibacterial properties, elongation at break of 279% and tensile strength of 2.23 MPa \[ [127](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR127 "Banerjee A, Roy P, Chakraborty J, Majumder M (2024) Environmentally degradable curcumin/ zinc oxide nanoparticles-incorporated polycaprolactone films for use as top-sheets in feminine sanitary hygiene napkins. Mater Today Commun 40:109452.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.mtcomm.2024.109452                                    ")\].
  
  ### Current work on the absorbent layer of sanitary pads
  
  #### Cellulose-pulp modification
  
  Most commercial disposable sanitary pads use SAPs that are not biodegradable and can cause harm to the skin. Therefore, Shibly _et. al._ looked into improving the traditional absorbing cellulose-pulp (cotton, viscose, and wood pulp) in the absorbing core of sanitary pads by combining it with two degradable biopolymers (sodium alginate, and carboxy methyl cellulose (CMC)) to create a sanitary pad without SAPs and with good performance for poor rural women \[ [87](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR87 "Shibly MDMH, Hossain MA, Hossain MF, Nur MG, Hossain MB (2021) Development of biopolymer-based menstrual pad and quality analysis against commercial merchandise. Bull Natl Res Cent 45:50.                    https://doi.org/10.1186/s42269-021-00504-2                                    ")\]. Six templates were designed and tested against three commercial sanitary pads. The results showed that a template containing sodium alginate in combination with CMC embedded in cellulosic fibres had the best comparable results to the best performing commercial pad tested on total saline absorptivity (63.2 g and 63.6 g), wicking height (8.85 cm and 9.01 cm), strike through (3 s and 3 s), retention capacity (32.6% and 36.1%), and physical characteristics (5.11 mm and 1.54 mm thickness), respectively. They concluded that disposable sanitary pads without commercial SAPs are more affordable alternatives, and that it is safe for the health of women and the environment \[ [87](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR87 "Shibly MDMH, Hossain MA, Hossain MF, Nur MG, Hossain MB (2021) Development of biopolymer-based menstrual pad and quality analysis against commercial merchandise. Bull Natl Res Cent 45:50.                    https://doi.org/10.1186/s42269-021-00504-2                                    ")\].
  
  Kipchumba _et. al._ conducted a similar study on possible alternatives for the absorbent layer of pads, to help girls in low income areas of Kenya to have access to affordable and reliable disposable sanitary pads, that are not harmful to the environment and that will enable them to attend school during menstruation \[ [128](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR128 "Kipchumba BB, Kulei AK, Mwasiagi JI (2023) The study and optimization of the hygroscopic properties of selected natural products with an aim of designing a sanitary pad suitable for low- and middle-income population. Cogent Eng 10:2196812.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/23311916.2023.2196812                                    ")\]. His study also incorporated the sustainability of the raw materials used and the manufacturing process. Pulp made from maize stalks and water hyacinth as the absorbent layer was extracted and tested, since it is readily available in Kenya. A 1:1 combination of the two absorbent fibres, showed the possibility of being a good affordable alternative in pads as the performance to a commercial pad containing polymers were comparable. He tested the retention capacity (55% and 75% after 24 h), retention capacity under pressure (50% and 60%), and absorption rate (1 min 46 s and 50 s), respectively. The manufacturing process conditions are mild as 2% and 0.5% NaOH solutions are used for the extraction of pulp from the maize stalks and hyacinth, respectively, and H2O2 for bleaching. Even though this study did not investigate the possible health risks, such as dermatitis and anti-microbial properties of the new raw materials, it addressed a deeper issue that is not only applicable to Kenya, but many low income populations across the globe. The lack of use of locally available raw materials in disposable sanitary pads that will result in more affordable pads, portrays a need in low income populations for pads that are reliable and convenient for use in areas where sufficient disposable facilities are lacking \[ [128](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR128 "Kipchumba BB, Kulei AK, Mwasiagi JI (2023) The study and optimization of the hygroscopic properties of selected natural products with an aim of designing a sanitary pad suitable for low- and middle-income population. Cogent Eng 10:2196812.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/23311916.2023.2196812                                    ")\].
  
  The same issues are being addressed in several similar studies where low income populations have a need for lower-priced, biodegradable, and comfortable sanitary pads that will not compromise their health and hygiene. Replacements such as recycled cellulose \[ [129](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR129 "Dhinakaran M, Senthil Kumar C, Sathis Kumar T (2017) Development and characterisation of sanitary napkins with lyocell / modal as absorbent core. Int Res J Eng Technol 4:1003–1006")\], banana fibres \[ [116](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR116 "Mukhila R, Amutha K (2023) Eco friendly disposable sanitary napkin development. Int J Res Publ Rev 4:2687–2693"), [130](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR130 "Petchimuthu P, Petchimuthu R, Basha SA, Murugan RK, Ganapathy HS, Durairaj UM (2019) Production of cost effective, biodegradable, disposable feminine sanitary napkins using banana fibres. Int J Eng Adv Technol 9:789–791.                    https://doi.org/10.35940/ijeat.A1145.1291S419                                    "), [131](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR131 "Banappagoudar SB, Mayank R, Kripa N, Kanna KR, Kurian NK (2021) Anti-bacterial sanitary napkin using biomaterial application. Nat Volatiles Essent Oils 8:12254–12263")\], milkweed fibres \[ [132](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR132 "Rajesh Kumar C, Raja D, Kumar SKS, Prakash C (2022) Study on moisture behavior properties of milkweed and milkweed/cotton blended sanitary napkins. J Nat Fibers 19:2849–2860.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/15440478.2020.1826384                                    ")\], waste flax yarn \[ [133](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR133 "Liu H, Zhang Y, Yao J (2014) Preparation and properties of an eco-friendly superabsorbent based on flax yarn waste for sanitary napkin applications. Fibers Polym 15:145–152.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s12221-014-0145-8                                    ")\], cotton waste from weaving factories \[ [134](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR134 "Sathishkumar G, Aarthi M, Senthilkumar R, Nithiya P, Selvakumar R, Bhattacharyya A (2019) Biodegradable cellulosic sanitary napkins from waste cotton and natural extract based anti-bacterial nanocolorants. J Indian Inst Sci 99:519–528.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s41745-019-00123-x                                    ")\], kenaf \[ [115](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR115 "Niveda R, Ramakrishnan G (2024) Development of cost-effective, eco-friendly sanitary pads for better health and sanitation of rural women. Funct Text Cloth 2023:289–294")\], cattail leaf fibres \[ [118](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR118 "Dewar-Norosky M, Wyllie-Runner J, Duguay N, Pries M, McRae C (2023) Design of a compostable menstrual pad: fluid acquisition layer and absorbent core. University of Manitoba")\], and bamboo \[ [113](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR113 "Mounika K, Ilango KB, Baranitharan AK, Dhivyatharshini S, Gowtham K, Swetha V, Vijaykumar S (2023) Biodegradable sanitary napkins-an innovative approach towards menstrual hygiene using bamboo fiber and lemon grass. Int J Pharm Sci 1:135–139.                    https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10078084                                    ")\] were studied.
  
  #### Super absorbent polymers
  
  SAP technology is a popular research area; not only does it possess significant absorption properties but, its properties can be tuned for the controlled release of substances, such as fertilizers \[ [135](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR135 "Feng D, Bai B, Wang H, Suo Y (2017) Novel fabrication of biodegradable superabsorbent microspheres with diffusion barrier through thermo-chemical modification and their potential agriculture applications for water holding and sustained release of fertilizer. J Agric Food Chem 65:5896–5907.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.jafc.7b01849                                    ")\], and drug delivery systems \[ [136](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR136 "Safari JB, Bapolisi AM, Krause RWM (2021) Development of pH-sensitive chitosan-g-poly(acrylamide-co-acrylic acid) hydrogel for controlled drug delivery of tenofovir disoproxil fumarate. Polymers (Basel) 13:3571.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/polym13203571                                    ")\]. Furthermore, its use in horticulture \[ [137](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR137 "Kim SM, Rhie YH, Kong SM, Kim YS, Na YH (2022) Synthesis of nanocomposite hydrogels for improved water retention in horticultural soil.   **Figure 15**
  
  ![Figure 15](https://media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1007%2Fs10853-025-11151-7/MediaObjects/10853_2025_11151_Fig15_HTML.png)The alternative text for this image may have been generated using AI.
  
  [Full size image](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/figures/15)
  
  Structures of natural polysaccharide polymers as illustrated by Ali et. al. \[ [162](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR162 "Ali A, Ahmed S (2018) Recent advances in edible polymer based hydrogels as a sustainable alternative to conventional polymers. J Agric Food Chem 66:6940–6967")\]. Reprinted from the Journal of Agricultural and Food Chemistry, 66, Ali A, Ahmed S, Recent Advances in Edible Polymer Based Hydrogels as a Sustainable Alternative to Conventional Polymers, Copyright (2018), with permission from American Chemical Society.
  
  To date, a menstrual pad traditionally consists of a cellulose-based absorbent layer between a permeable top sheet and a waterproof back sheet. For use in sanitary pads, SAPs are commonly made in the form of small spheres and integrated into the cellulose absorbent layer. However, Woeller _et. al._ designed and extensively tested a non-cellulosic absorbent foam core with the aim of reducing irritation, sensitization, and improve skin compatibility with the sanitary pad \[ [17](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR17 "Woeller KE, Hochwalt AE (2015) Safety assessment of sanitary pads with a polymeric foam absorbent core. Regul Toxicol Pharmacol 73:419–424.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.yrtph.2015.07.028                                    ")\].
  
  The absorbent foam core made by Woeller _et. al._ is made from high molecular weight polymers that are biologically inert and non-bioavailable, small amounts of emulsifier, and solvent \[ [17](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR17 "Woeller KE, Hochwalt AE (2015) Safety assessment of sanitary pads with a polymeric foam absorbent core. Regul Toxicol Pharmacol 73:419–424.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.yrtph.2015.07.028                                    ")\]. Since the 3D structure of foams are so rigid, the reflux probability of the high molecular weight polymer is negligible. However, smaller monomer by-products or residuals may pose a risk, thus they performed extractions on the foam with cyclohexane and solvents that mimic menstrual conditions which revealed.
  
  < 200 ppm and < 7 ppm of residuals, respectively. Proving that even the worst case exposure of reflux components poses no significant risk \[ [17](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR17 "Woeller KE, Hochwalt AE (2015) Safety assessment of sanitary pads with a polymeric foam absorbent core. Regul Toxicol Pharmacol 73:419–424.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.yrtph.2015.07.028                                    ")\].
  
  The absorbent foam was also subject to non-clinic tests which included cytotoxicity tests on murine L-929 cells, a 5-day vaginal irritation study in rabbits, and mouse lymph node sensitization tests. Skin compatibility tests included a 5-day irritation study of the finished product, a 4-day test on sensitive and normal skin of the finished product, a 21-day test on the absorbent foam core, and skin sensitization tests. Results showed that the foam was significantly less irritating than the raw materials themselves. Overall, the systemic evaluation revealed that the finished product confirms skin compatibility showcasing that foam absorbent cores made from high molecular weight polymers may prove as a good alternative to reduce the risk of skin dermatitis and improve the health and hygiene of women \[ [17](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR17 "Woeller KE, Hochwalt AE (2015) Safety assessment of sanitary pads with a polymeric foam absorbent core. Regul Toxicol Pharmacol 73:419–424.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.yrtph.2015.07.028                                    ")\].
  
  Chitosan is a natural polymer that is second most abundant to cellulose, and is found in crustacean exoskeletons, insect cuticles, and fungi cell walls \[ [163](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR163 "Mistry PA, Konar MN, Latha S, Chadha U, Bhardwaj P, Eticha TK (2023) Chitosan superabsorbent biopolymers in sanitary and hygiene applications. Int J Polym Sci 2023:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2023/4717905                                    ")\]. This linear polysaccharide (see Fig. [15](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig15)) exhibits anti-microbial activity due to its alternating N and OH groups, is biodegradable due to its 1,4-glycosidic bonds, bio-compatible, non-toxic, and non-volatile, and the FDA has already approved it for food packaging, making it a popular option for eco-friendly polymeric products \[ [163](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR163 "Mistry PA, Konar MN, Latha S, Chadha U, Bhardwaj P, Eticha TK (2023) Chitosan superabsorbent biopolymers in sanitary and hygiene applications. Int J Polym Sci 2023:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2023/4717905                                    ")\].
  
  Mistry _et. al._ reviewed the issues related to inorganic sanitary products and how adding chitosan in SAPs can improve biodegradability and health hazards associated with absorbent hygiene products. The properties of chitosan such as water retention capacity, swelling ability, its applications, biodegradability, different approaches in synthesizing SAPs for pads, and its anti-microbial activity were discussed. Their review found that chitosan in its pure form has limited applications and poor mechanical properties. However, if its modified, it has a wide domain of applications and exhibits super-absorbency, maintains good biodegradability, and maintains its anti-microbial properties \[ [163](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR163 "Mistry PA, Konar MN, Latha S, Chadha U, Bhardwaj P, Eticha TK (2023) Chitosan superabsorbent biopolymers in sanitary and hygiene applications. Int J Polym Sci 2023:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2023/4717905                                    ")\]. Its uses expand from SAPs to applications in the textile industry, in food packaging, in wound dressings, implants, and many more \[ [163](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR163 "Mistry PA, Konar MN, Latha S, Chadha U, Bhardwaj P, Eticha TK (2023) Chitosan superabsorbent biopolymers in sanitary and hygiene applications. Int J Polym Sci 2023:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2023/4717905                                    ")\]. They found a gap in the research done on chitosan related SAPs, which includes the recyclability of these products, as recyclability of chitosan based sanitary pads will be an even better property and boost its popularity \[ [163](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR163 "Mistry PA, Konar MN, Latha S, Chadha U, Bhardwaj P, Eticha TK (2023) Chitosan superabsorbent biopolymers in sanitary and hygiene applications. Int J Polym Sci 2023:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2023/4717905                                    ")\].
  
  A superabsorbent hydrogel with chitosan, acrylamide, and itaconic acid (IA) was prepared via radical polymerization by Dan _et. al._ as a natural-based hydrogel \[ [164](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR164 "Dan S, Banivaheb S, Hashemipour H, Kalantari M (2021) Synthesis, characterization and absorption study of chitosan-g-poly(acrylamide-co-itaconic acid) hydrogel. Polym Bull 78:1887–1907.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s00289-020-03190-8                                    ")\]. Acrylamide is a copolymer that improves the hydrophilicity and water absorbency of a polymer. Itaconic acid is a functional group that can contribute to pH-sensitive absorbance. As its acid groups are ionized, repulsive forces will uncoil the polymer chains allowing for the increased uptake of fluid into the polymer network. At optimum conditions, the hydrogel has a maximum absorption of 42.46 g.g−1 of distilled water \[ [164](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR164 "Dan S, Banivaheb S, Hashemipour H, Kalantari M (2021) Synthesis, characterization and absorption study of chitosan-g-poly(acrylamide-co-itaconic acid) hydrogel. Polym Bull 78:1887–1907.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s00289-020-03190-8                                    ")\].
  
  To address the challenge of nonbiodegradable SAPs and the eco-unfriendly method to synthesize them, Alam _et. al._ designed a biodegradable cellulose-chitosan cross-linked SAP where water is the only by-product during synthesis. Oxidized cellulose polymers are reacted with carboxymethyl chitosan by Schiff base reaction to produce a crosslinked hydrogel. The best performing hydrogel containing 25% modified chitosan absorbs 610 g.g−1 and 85 g.g−1 of distilled and saline water, respectively. The absorption of saline solutions containing ammonium and potassium ions increased to 91 g.g−1 and 96 g.g−1, respectively. The hydrogel displayed good reusability by losing only 5—10% of reswelling capacity after 4 consecutive absorptions. These superior absorption properties, the reusability, and biodegradable nature may prove to be a good replacement for petroleum-based SAPs \[ [165](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR165 "Alam MN, Christopher LP (2018) Natural cellulose-chitosan cross-linked superabsorbent hydrogels with superior swelling properties. ACS Sustain Chem Eng 6:8736–8742.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acssuschemeng.8b01062                                    ")\].
  
  Since most SAPs lack antimicrobial activity, Fang _et. al._ sought to create a chitosan-based SAP with high water absorption and retention capacities, while retaining its antimicrobial activity (see Fig. [16](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig16)) \[ [39](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR39 "Fang S, Wang G, Xing R, Chen X, Liu S, Qin Y, Li K, Wang X, Li R, Li P (2019) Synthesis of superabsorbent polymers based on chitosan derivative graft acrylic acid-co-acrylamide and its property testing. Int J Biol Macromol 132:575–584.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2019.03.176                                    ")\]. Water absorption capacity tests showed excellent water absorption of 615 g.g−1 and saline solution absorption of 44 g.g−1. The SAP was subjected to antibacterial tests, where it showed 66% and 71% inhibition of _E. coli_ and _S. aureus_, respectively. The presence of the quaternary amino groups on the chitosan backbone was responsible for the good results \[ [39](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR39 "Fang S, Wang G, Xing R, Chen X, Liu S, Qin Y, Li K, Wang X, Li R, Li P (2019) Synthesis of superabsorbent polymers based on chitosan derivative graft acrylic acid-co-acrylamide and its property testing. Int J Biol Macromol 132:575–584.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2019.03.176                                    ")\].

Copyright (2019), with permission from Elsevier.
  
  The growing demand for sustainable and biodegradable hygiene products has prompted Czarnecka _et. al._ to develop a SAP with carboxymethyl starch (CMS) and chitosan (CT) crosslinked with vanillin (Van) by Schiff base reaction \[ [166](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR166 "Czarnecka E, Nowaczyk J, Prochoń M, Masek A (2022) Nanoarchitectonics for biodegradable superabsorbent based on carboxymethyl starch and chitosan cross-linked with vanillin. Int J Mol Sci 23:5386.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms23105386                                    ")\]. The SEM images (shown in Fig. [17](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig17)) clearly show the difference a crosslinking agent makes to a SAP, as it alters the morphology of the SAP by creating cracks and holes on the surface of the SAP. Therefore, diffusion of fluids into the interior of the SAP network increases. This was confirmed with absorption studies where sample CMS-CT-Van(0.04) had the highest absorption capacity with ~ 70 g.g−1 and 183.9 g.g−1 absorption of saline solution and water, respectively \[ [166](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR166 "Czarnecka E, Nowaczyk J, Prochoń M, Masek A (2022) Nanoarchitectonics for biodegradable superabsorbent based on carboxymethyl starch and chitosan cross-linked with vanillin. Int J Mol Sci 23:5386.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms23105386                                    ")\].

Int J Mol Sci 23:5386.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms23105386                                    ")\]. Reprinted from the Int J Mol Sci, 23, Czarnecka E, Nowaczyk J, Prochoń M, Masek A, Nanoarchitectonics for Biodegradable Superabsorbent Based on Carboxymethyl Starch and Chitosan Cross‐Linked with Vanillin, Copyright (2022), with permission from MDPI.
  
  Cellulose-based hydrogels have also been reviewed by Bashari _et. al._ as a good biodegradable absorbent material \[ [25](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR25 "Bashari A, Rouhani Shirvan A, Shakeri M (2018) Cellulose-based hydrogels for personal care products. Polym Adv Technol 29:2853–2867")\]. Cellulose has an abundant collection of hydroxyl groups that participate in water absorption by forming hydrogen bonds with the molecules. This natural polymer’s swelling and absorption capacity can be controlled with the type and degree of crosslinkers used to make the SAP \[ [25](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR25 "Bashari A, Rouhani Shirvan A, Shakeri M (2018) Cellulose-based hydrogels for personal care products. Polym Adv Technol 29:2853–2867")\]. Despite the advantages and disadvantages of using natural polymers such as cellulose, they mostly have some of the properties required by an ideal SAP to be used in AHPs, which are listed in Table [8](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab8).
  
  **Table 8 Biodegradable SAPs made**
  
  [Full size table](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/tables/8)
  
  Capezza _et. al._ investigated a simple, low cost, non-toxic, and sustainable method to produce bio-based SAPs from potato protein \[ [167](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR167 "Capezza AJ, Glad D, Özeren HD, Newson WR, Olsson RT, Johansson E, Hedenqvist MS (2019) Novel sustainable superabsorbents: a one-pot method for functionalization of side-stream potato proteins. ACS Sustain Chem Eng 7:18048–18054.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acssuschemeng.9b04352                                    ")\]. They used five different acylating agents to modify the potato proteins, which were subjected to absorption tests. The best absorption results were obtained for their succinic anhydride functionalized potato protein concentrate with a 600, 60, and 530% absorption capacity of water, saline, and blood solutions. The blood swelling capacity is reportedly more than 50% of that of commercial fossil-based SAPs used in disposable sanitary pads _;_ showing good progress in creating inexpensive and sustainable SAPs for absorbent hygiene products. Additionally, they developed a scalable one-pot synthesis method where functionalization and material formation happen in the same reactor \[ [167](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR167 "Capezza AJ, Glad D, Özeren HD, Newson WR, Olsson RT, Johansson E, Hedenqvist MS (2019) Novel sustainable superabsorbents: a one-pot method for functionalization of side-stream potato proteins. ACS Sustain Chem Eng 7:18048–18054.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acssuschemeng.9b04352                                    ")\]. The same research team investigated the same properties of functionalized (acylated) wheat gluten proteins, since wheat protein is also a sustainable resource. The absorption results were 22, 5, and 3 g.g−1 of water, saline, and blood solution, respectively \[ [168](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR168 "Capezza AJ, Lundman M, Olsson RT, Newson WR, Hedenqvist MS, Johansson E (2020) Carboxylated wheat gluten proteins: a green solution for production of sustainable superabsorbent materials. Biomacromol 21:1709–1719.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.9b01646                                    ")\].
  
  They also showed that it is possible to make absorbent foams (see Fig. [18](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig18)) from the same wheat gluten protein with maximum absorption of 25 g.g−1 and.
  
  **Figure 18**
  
  ![Figure 18](https://media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1007%2Fs10853-025-11151-7/MediaObjects/10853_2025_11151_Fig18_HTML.jpg)The alternative text for this image may have been generated using AI.
  
  [Full size image](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/figures/18)
  
  SEM images of the foams made with wheat gluten proteins \[ [169](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR169 "Capezza AJ, Wu Q, Newson WR, Olsson RT, Espuche E, Johansson E, Hedenqvist MS (2019) Superabsorbent and fully biobased protein foams with a natural cross-linker and cellulose nanofibers. ACS Omega 4:18257–18267.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.9b02271                                    ")\]. Reprinted from American Chemical Society Omega, 4, Capezza AJ, Wu Q, Newson WR, Olsson RT, Espuche E, Johansson E, Hedenqvist MS, Superabsorbent and Fully Biobased Protein Foams with a Natural Cross-Linker and Cellulose Nanofibers, Copyright (2019), with permission from American Chemical Society.
  
  7 g.g−1 of water and blood solution, respectively. The wheat gluten was mechanically foamed while allowing it to undergo intermolecular crosslinking at 90 °C, after which it was freeze dried. The blood swelling capacity was 50% of the absorption capacity of commercial foams taken from disposable sanitary pads \[ [169](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR169 "Capezza AJ, Wu Q, Newson WR, Olsson RT, Espuche E, Johansson E, Hedenqvist MS (2019) Superabsorbent and fully biobased protein foams with a natural cross-linker and cellulose nanofibers. ACS Omega 4:18257–18267.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.9b02271                                    ")\].
  
  Another problem associated with SAPs, whether it be in the agricultural or hygiene sectors, is that good retention capacities and mechanical properties are often lacking. Thus, Menceloglu _et. al._ designed a nanocomposite hydrogel to improve these properties \[ [170](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR170 "Menceloǧlu Y, Menceloǧlu YZ, Seven SA (2022) Triblock superabsorbent polymer nanocomposites with enhanced water retention capacities and rheological characteristics. ACS Omega 7:20486–20494.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.1c06961                                    ")\]. Their SAP was made from acrylic acid, acrylamide, and 2-acrylamido-2-methylpropane-1-sulfonic acid monomeric units crosslinked by vinyltrimethoxysilane. The SAPs were loaded halloysite nanotubes to tune swelling capacity and rheological properties. The maximum absorption of distilled water was 537 g.g−1 for the SAP with the most nanotubes present and least amount of crosslinking. The retention property was extended to 27 days, and the maximum storage modulus (~ 2700 Pa) was obtained in the SAP with the lowest concentration of nanotubes and the highest number of crosslinking. Therefore, the designed SAP displayed absorption that is indirectly proportional to crosslinking density, and mechanical properties that are directly proportional to crosslinking density \[ [170](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR170 "Menceloǧlu Y, Menceloǧlu YZ, Seven SA (2022) Triblock superabsorbent polymer nanocomposites with enhanced water retention capacities and rheological characteristics. ACS Omega 7:20486–20494.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.1c06961                                    ")\].
  
  The potential of itaconic acid (IA) as a candidate for making eco-friendly SAPs was investigated by Choi _et. al._ \[ [171](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR171 "Choi H, Park J, Lee J (2022) Sustainable bio-based superabsorbent polymer: poly(itaconic acid) with superior swelling properties. ACS Appl Polym Mater 4:4098–4108.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.2c00021                                    ")\]. IA is an organic acid produced by a fungus as a product from the thermal decomposition of citric acid. Choi _et. al._ created their poly(itaconic acid) (PIA) polymer through radical polymerization of the IA monomer using an initiator. To form the hydrogel, the PIA polymers were crosslinked with poly(ethylene glycol) diacrylate (PEGDA) and then dried in an oven \[ [171](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR171 "Choi H, Park J, Lee J (2022) Sustainable bio-based superabsorbent polymer: poly(itaconic acid) with superior swelling properties. ACS Appl Polym Mater 4:4098–4108.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.2c00021                                    ")\]. The maximum absorption in distilled water achieved was 370.7 g.g−1 and in a saline solution was 53 g.g−1 (see Table [8](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab8)). They attributed the low saline solution absorption to the reduction of repulsive forces between the carboxylate ions of different strands, due to the presence of sodium cations, resulting in a decreased osmotic pressure. Rewetting results showed that PIA can be reused repeatedly. This indicates that the polymer structure is protected and that the three-dimensional structure is very stable \[ [171](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR171 "Choi H, Park J, Lee J (2022) Sustainable bio-based superabsorbent polymer: poly(itaconic acid) with superior swelling properties. ACS Appl Polym Mater 4:4098–4108.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.2c00021                                    ")\].
  
  Bachra _et. al._ designed a novel SAP from carboxymethyl guar gum, bentonite, and silica, crosslinked with borax by an organic synthesis reported as the Williamson etherification reaction \[ [172](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR172 "Bachra Y, Grouli A, Damiri F, Zhu XX, Talbi M, Berrada M (2022) Synthesis, characterization, and swelling properties of a new highly absorbent hydrogel based on carboxymethyl guar gum reinforced with bentonite and silica particles for disposable hygiene products. ACS Omega 7:39002–39018.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.2c04744                                    ")\]. Guar Gum is extracted from seeds and is sold commercially in parts of Asia and North America. It is non-toxic, contains hydrophilic groups, is biodegradable, and is a renewable source for SAP production. Bentonite was added as a clay filler and silica was incorporated in the design to enhance water retention properties of the SAP. The water absorption results showed that this novel SAP has an absorption of ~ 42 g.g−1 of distilled water \[ [172](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR172 "Bachra Y, Grouli A, Damiri F, Zhu XX, Talbi M, Berrada M (2022) Synthesis, characterization, and swelling properties of a new highly absorbent hydrogel based on carboxymethyl guar gum reinforced with bentonite and silica particles for disposable hygiene products. ACS Omega 7:39002–39018.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.2c04744                                    ")\] (Fig. [19](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig19)).
  
  **Figure 19**

ACS Appl Polym Mater 4:4098–4108.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.2c00021                                    ")\] Reprinted from ACS Applied Polymer Materials, 4, Choi H, Park J, Lee J, Sustainable Bio-Based Superabsorbent Polymer: Poly(itaconic acid) with Superior Swelling Properties, Copyright (2022), with permission from American Chemical Society.
  
  It is believed that creating semisynthetic SAPs where petrochemical-based monomers are combined with polysaccharides will result in a biodegradable SAP. Fei _et. al._ used sodium lignosulfonate (SL) as their polysaccharide of choice, not only because this component is an environmentally friendly alternative, it is a cheaper and sustainable alternative with antibacterial properties and many polar and ionic groups that will make it a good absorber. Polyacrylic acid and SL were crosslinked with hyperbranched polyether crosslinkers (HPG), as shown in Fig. [20](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig20), by free radical graft copolymerization. By varying the ratios, the best absorption capacities achieved were 934 g.g−1 for distilled water and 91.6 g.g−1 for saline solutions. The structure and functional groups on SL (phenyl and conjugated system) creates more free space for water absorption. The presence of the quaternary ammonium group (− N+(CH3)3) on SL provided effective antibacterial properties with inhibition percentage between 85 – 100% depending on the SL concentration in the hydrogel. Fei _et. al_ managed to improve several characteristics of traditional hydrogels with sustainable and biodegradable polymers \[ [173](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR173 "Fei J, Tang T, Zhou L, He H, Ma M, Shi Y, Chen S, Wang X (2023) High-toughness and biodegradable superabsorbent hydrogels based on dual functional crosslinkers. ACS Appl Polym Mater 5:3686–3697.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.3c00348                                    ")\].
  
  **Figure 20**
  
  ![Figure 20](https://media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1007%2Fs10853-025-11151-7/MediaObjects/10853_2025_11151_Fig20_HTML.png)The alternative text for this image may have been generated using AI.

  SEM images of the hydrogels synthesised by Fei et. al. \[ [173](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR173 "Fei J, Tang T, Zhou L, He H, Ma M, Shi Y, Chen S, Wang X (2023) High-toughness and biodegradable superabsorbent hydrogels based on dual functional crosslinkers. ACS Appl Polym Mater 5:3686–3697.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.3c00348                                    ")\]. Reprinted from ACS Applied Polymer Materials, 5, Fei J, Tang T, Zhou L, He H, Ma M, Shi Y, Chen S, Wang X, High-Toughness and Biodegradable Superabsorbent Hydrogels Based on Dual Functional Crosslinkers, Copyright (2023), with permission from American Chemical Society.
  
  The research needed for SAP design and synthesis is very complex and time consuming. It requires an understanding of the SAP types, preparation, absorption mechanisms, swelling properties, and type of polymers used to improve SAP designs and properties. It is costly and requires costly resources. A review paper by Mogale and colleagues discussed this and suggested the incorporation of the aid of computational methods as a valuable solution \[ [174](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR174 "Mogale R, Schutte-Smith M, Erasmus E, De Wet K, Visser HG (2024) Toward sustainable menstrual health management: focus on super absorbent polymers. J Mater Sci 59:6138–6168.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s10853-024-09519-2                                    ")\]. A study where optimum crosslinking conditions were determined with computational methods for bio-based SAPs was highlighted \[ [175](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR175 "Kim H-C, Kwon Y-R, Kim J-S, Kwon M, Kim J-H, Kim D-H (2022) Computational approach to the surface-crosslinking process of superabsorbent polymer via central composite design. Polymers (Basel) 14:3842.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/polym14183842                                    ")\]. Another study exhibited the success in designing a SAP specifically for waste water treatment containing toxic metals \[ [176](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR176 "Savaskan Yilmaz S, Yildirim N, Misir M, Misirlioglu Y, Celik E (2020) Synthesis, characterization of a new polyacrylic acid superabsorbent, some heavy metal ion sorption, the adsorption isotherms, and quantum chemical investigation. Materials 13:4390.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/ma13194390                                    ")\]. Mogale suggested it is worthwhile to consider the aid of computational chemistry in polymer chemistry such as SAPs for reduced operational costs \[ [174](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR174 "Mogale R, Schutte-Smith M, Erasmus E, De Wet K, Visser HG (2024) Toward sustainable menstrual health management: focus on super absorbent polymers. J Mater Sci 59:6138–6168.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s10853-024-09519-2                                    ")\]. This new research avenue, if applied to sanitary napkins, has the potential to ultimately improve sanitary wear hygiene, the impact it will have on the environment, and its cost effectiveness.
  
  Incorporating nanotechnology into the design and synthesis of SAPs is another exciting research topic. Silica nanoparticles was used in a carboxymethyl guar gum-bentonite hydrogel to produce a hydrogel with highly absorbent properties, stability, and enhanced retention capacity, by Bachra et. al. \[ [172](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR172 "Bachra Y, Grouli A, Damiri F, Zhu XX, Talbi M, Berrada M (2022) Synthesis, characterization, and swelling properties of a new highly absorbent hydrogel based on carboxymethyl guar gum reinforced with bentonite and silica particles for disposable hygiene products. ACS Omega 7:39002–39018.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.2c04744                                    ")\]. Bayat et. al. found that by incorporating silver nanoparticles in their chitosan-SAP, the nanoparticles reduced crosslinking of the chitosan chain, increasing the free volume in the polymer network, resulting in better absorption \[ [177](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR177 "Bayat Tork M, Hemmati Nejad N, Ghalehbagh S, Bashari A, Shakeri-Zadeh A, Kamrava S (2016) In situ green synthesis of silver nanoparticles/chitosan/poly vinyl alcohol/poly ethylene glycol hydrogel nanocomposite for novel finishing of nasal tampons. J Ind Text 45:1399–1416.                    https://doi.org/10.1177/1528083714560255                                    ")\]. It is evident that it has the potential to enhance the absorption properties of SAPs with various mechanisms.
  
  Bettelli et. al. researched and developed an absorbing glycerol-plasticized wheat gluten biofoam, that is produced by extrusion. Extrusion is a popular process used in industry to process polymers. They designed an energy efficient upscalable extrusion process whereby the biofoam is extruded at a low temperature of 70 °C, just enough for their blowing agent to create stable pores and for the wheat gluten protein to denature and form new covalent crosslinks. The process is sustainable as the extrusion mixture only requires the inexpensive, readily available wheat gluten powder mixed with glycerol and the blowing agent. The biofoam with wheat gluten:glycerol:ammonium bicarbonate weight ratio 65%:30%:5% achieved a saline absorption capacity of 140% after 24 h \[ [178](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR178 "Bettelli MA, Capezza AJ, Nilsson F, Johansson E, Olsson RT, Hedenqvist MS (2022) Sustainable wheat protein biofoams: dry upscalable extrusion at low temperature. Biomacromol 23:5116–5126.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.2c00953                                    ")\]. The effects of other scalable preparation methods on a similar wheat gluten biofoam were also investigated \[ [179](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR179 "Bettelli MA, Traissac E, Latras A, Rosado MJ, Guerrero A, Olsson RT, Hedenqvist MS, Capezza AJ (2024) Eco-friendly disposable porous absorbents from gluten proteins through diverse plastic processing techniques. J Clean Prod 459:142419.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jclepro.2024.142419                                    ")\]. The oven expansion biofoam with a wheat gluten:glycerol;ammonium bicarbonate weight ratio of 70%:30%:5%, compared to a compression moulding and an extrusion biofoam, seemed to perform the best, with saline (after 24 h) and blood absorption (after 30 min) capacities of 3.5 – 3 g/g and 2.1 g/g, respectively. This study highlighted the importance of shear forces and temperature during biofoam production \[ [179](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR179 "Bettelli MA, Traissac E, Latras A, Rosado MJ, Guerrero A, Olsson RT, Hedenqvist MS, Capezza AJ (2024) Eco-friendly disposable porous absorbents from gluten proteins through diverse plastic processing techniques. J Clean Prod 459:142419.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jclepro.2024.142419                                    ")\]. The effect of additives, such as citric acid, gallic acid, and genipin crosslinkers, was investigated on a biofoam with the same wheat gluten:glycerol:ammonium bicarbonate ratio. The results showed that the biofoam with the lowest citric acid and gallic acid concentrations (1 wt% per 100 g of wheat gluten-glycerol solution) performed the best, with a maximum saline absorption capacity of 130% that was achieved \[ [180](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR180 "Bettelli MA, Hu Q, Capezza AJ, Johansson E, Olsson RT, Hedenqvist MS (2024) Effects of multi-functional additives during foam extrusion of wheat gluten materials. Commun Chem 7:75.                    https://doi.org/10.1038/s42004-024-01150-1                                    ")\]. End-of-life studies were done on biofoams with the same wheat gluten:glycerol:ammonium bicarbonate:additive ratios. Results showed the foams underwent more the 80% biodegradation in soil (moistened basic soil with industrial fertilizer kept at 25 °C), where the amount of degradation depends on the additive. In an aqueous alkaline solution, complete degradation was achieved after 5 weeks. Further testing showed that the biofoams can be used as fertilizer and can be recycled into films with compression moulding \[ [181](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR181 "Bettelli MA, Perdigón LA, Zhao L, Pereira PFM, Jiménez-Quero A, Capezza AJ, Prade T, Johansson E, Olsson RT, Hedenqvist MS, Sabino MA (2025) Biodegradation, bioassimilation and recycling properties of wheat gluten foams. ACS Agric Sci Technol 5:805–821.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsagscitech.4c00798                                    ")\].
  
  More recently, Latras _et. al._ used an oven expansion technique to test the effect of the density and porosity of a wheat gluten-glycerol (2:1 weight ratio) foam on saline and blood absorption capacity. The maximum absorption capacities of 4.5 g/g and 1 g/g, after 30 min were achieved with the foam that was prepared at 120 °C with sodium bicarbonate as blowing agent (see SEM image in Fig. [21](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig21)) \[ [182](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR182 "Latras A, Bettelli MA, Pereira PFM, Jiménez-Quero A, Hedenqvist MS, Capezza AJ (2025) Assessing the properties of protein foams as an alternative absorbent core layer in disposable sanitary pads. RSC Appl Polym 3:438–452.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D4LP00323C                                    ")\]. These studies revealed that the processing conditions, foam structure, and additive type significantly influence the biofoam’s performance. It demonstrated promising absorption capacities, high biodegradability, and potential for recycling and agricultural use, highlighting their promise as eco-friendly alternatives for absorbent and disposable materials.

RSC Appl Polym 3:438–452.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D4LP00323C                                    ")\] Reprinted from RSC Applied Polymers Journal, 3, Latras A, Bettelli MA, Pereira PFM, Jiménez-Quero A, Hedenqvist MS, Capezza AJ, Assessing the properties of protein foams as an alternative absorbent core layer in disposable sanitary pads,438–452, under the Creative Commons Agreement (2025).
  
  In an attempt to design a biofoam with manufacturing properties attractive to industry and with the potential to replace petroleum-based absorbents in commercial sanitary pads, Jugé _et. al._ designed an extrusion method with wheat gluten and zein protein as a bioblend. They did an extensive study where several factors were tested being: wheat gluten to zein protein ratio, glycerol concentration, water content, extrusion temperature and speed, and blowing agent content. Additionally, hydrolytic degradation studies were also done. They formed pad prototypes by compressing extruded pellets to form an absorbent core and by extruding porous films as a nonwoven top sheet replacement. The maximum saline absorption capacity achieved by extrudates was 3.5 g/g within 10 min, after which a decrease in capacity was recorded. Figure [22](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig22) shows a SEM image of a cross section of one of the extrudates tested. Under load (0.25 psi, a maximum absorption capacity of 0.6 g/g was observed. However, the pad prototype showed absorption capacities below 0.5 g.g for saline and blood solutions. Instead, visual absorption tests showed, by grinding the extrudates a better absorption will be achieved. The biofoams showed more than 70% degradation after 5 weeks in aqueous alkaline conditions \[ [183](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR183 "Jugé A, Moreno-Villafranca J, Perez-Puyana VM, Jiménez-Rosado M, Sabino M, Capezza AJ (2023) Porous thermoformed protein bioblends as degradable absorbent alternatives in sanitary materials. ACS Appl Polym Mater 5:6976–6989.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.3c01027                                    ")\].

  Cross sectional SEM image of the best performing biofoam designed by Jugé et. al \[ [183](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR183 "Jugé A, Moreno-Villafranca J, Perez-Puyana VM, Jiménez-Rosado M, Sabino M, Capezza AJ (2023) Porous thermoformed protein bioblends as degradable absorbent alternatives in sanitary materials. ACS Appl Polym Mater 5:6976–6989.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.3c01027                                    ")\]. Reprinted from ACS Applied Polymer Materials, 5, Jugé A, Moreno-Villafranca J, Perez-Puyana V, Jiménez-Rosado M, Sabino M, Capezza A, Porous Thermoformed Protein Bioblends as Degradable Absorbent Alternatives in Sanitary Materials, 6979–6989, under the Creative Commons Agreement (2023).
  
  It is evident that the possibilities of using polysaccharides and proteins to form SAPs are almost endless, with different crosslinkers, and grafting and polymerization methods. Esterification with carboxylic acids \[ [184](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR184 "Afolabi TA (2019) Synthesis of biodegradable superabsorbent hydrogel from carboxymetylated cassava starch for use in sanitary pads. J Chem Soc Nigeria 44:433–452")\], Schiff base reactions with aldehydes \[ [165](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR165 "Alam MN, Christopher LP (2018) Natural cellulose-chitosan cross-linked superabsorbent hydrogels with superior swelling properties. ACS Sustain Chem Eng 6:8736–8742.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acssuschemeng.8b01062                                    ")\], radical polymerization \[ [158](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR158 "Fang S, Wang G, Li P, Xing R, Liu S, Qin Y, Yu H, Chen X, Li K (2018) Synthesis of chitosan derivative graft acrylic acid superabsorbent polymers and its application as water retaining agent. Int J Biol Macromol 115:754–761.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2018.04.072                                    ")\], and ionic interactions with metals \[ [185](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR185 "Orhan B, Kaygusuz H, Erim FB (2022) Sustainable alginate-carboxymethyl cellulose superabsorbents prepared by a novel quasi-cryogelation method. J Polym Res 29:333.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s10965-022-03185-1                                    ")\] are popular crosslinking techniques. Many of these methods are not scale-up friendly as neutralization, washing with solvents such as ethanol and methanol are required, and many employ freeze-drying as a foaming/drying step. The radiation methods reported here are interesting techniques that may show promise in industry as no extra chemicals are needed to initiate the polymerization process, and washing and neutralizations steps are alleviated \[ [186](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR186 "Amaraweera SM, Gunathilake C, Gunawardene OHP, Dassanayake RS, Fernando NML, Wanninayaka DB, Rajapaksha SM, Manamperi A, Gangoda M, Manchanda A, Fernando CAN, Kulatunga AK, Manipura A (2022) Preparation and characterization of dual-modified cassava starch-based biodegradable foams for sustainable packaging applications. ACS Omega 7:19579–19590.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.2c01292                                    ")\]. The extrusion methods discussed here are scalable and show promising results that go full circle, from simple preparation methods, industrially attractive manufacturing techniques, producing foams with promising absorption, recycling, and biodegradation capabilities \[ [182](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR182 "Latras A, Bettelli MA, Pereira PFM, Jiménez-Quero A, Hedenqvist MS, Capezza AJ (2025) Assessing the properties of protein foams as an alternative absorbent core layer in disposable sanitary pads. RSC Appl Polym 3:438–452.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D4LP00323C                                    "), [183](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR183 "Jugé A, Moreno-Villafranca J, Perez-Puyana VM, Jiménez-Rosado M, Sabino M, Capezza AJ (2023) Porous thermoformed protein bioblends as degradable absorbent alternatives in sanitary materials. ACS Appl Polym Mater 5:6976–6989.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsapm.3c01027                                    ")\].
  
  #### Current work on biodegradable superabsorbent fibres (SAFs)
  
  Most commercial SAPs have low surface areas due to the spherical or _pseudo_-spherical shapes of the granules. Electrospinning hydrogels into fine fibres, in the micron- and nano-size, is a technique receiving increased attention to improve surface area to volume ratios. Another challenge experienced, especially with acryl-based SAPs is the low strength of the shape of the SAP when absorbing water. Improving the strength of a swelled hydrogel can be done by preparing them as nanocomposites, interpenetrated polymer networks or surface cross linking. This is achieved by electrospinning \[ [31](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR31 "Tang KPM, Li CH, Kan CW (2020) Instrumentation for measuring the wet frictional property of sanitary pads. Fibers Polym 21:216–223.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s12221-020-9623-3                                    ")\].
  
  Zhang _et. al._ designed pH responsive absorbing electrospun fibres from chitosan and acrylic acid \[ [194](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR194 "Zhang R-Y, Zaslavski E, Vasilyev G, Boas M, Zussman E (2018) Tunable pH-responsive chitosan-poly(acrylic acid) electrospun fibers. Biomacromol 19:588–595.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.7b01672                                    ")\]. The degree of deacetylation (DDA), the charge of chitosan (cationic), and the charge of acrylic acid (anionic) formed a pH responsive system where absorption at a pH < 2.4 is ~ 2200% and at a pH > 2.4 is ~ 250% in distilled water (see Table [9](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab9)) \[ [194](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR194 "Zhang R-Y, Zaslavski E, Vasilyev G, Boas M, Zussman E (2018) Tunable pH-responsive chitosan-poly(acrylic acid) electrospun fibers. Biomacromol 19:588–595.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.7b01672                                    ")\]. Figure [23](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig23) shows SEM images of the resultant SAFs with different degrees of deacetylation of chitosan.

Biomacromol 19:588–595.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.7b01672                                    ")\].   Chen _et. al._ designed a SAP with gelatine and polylactic acid copolymer crosslinked with hyaluronic acid \[ [195](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR195 "Chen W, Chen S, Morsi Y, El-Hamshary H, El-Newhy M, Fan C, Mo X (2016) Superabsorbent 3D scaffold based on electrospun nanofibers for cartilage tissue engineering. ACS Appl Mater Interfaces 8:24415–24425.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.6b06825                                    ")\]. After creating electrospun fibres from this SAP, the fibres were shredded, homogenized, and poured into moulds, freeze dried and crosslinked with N-\[3-(dimethylamino)propyl\]-N′-ethylcarbodiimide hydrochloride (EDC). This SAF, as shown in Fig. [24](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig24), achieved an absorption percentage of 1300% in distilled water within 5 min, and maintained this absorption capacity even after six compressions. Even though this three-dimensional scaffold was designed for tissue engineering, the excellent absorption capacity, structural stability, and its non-toxicity are attractive properties to sanitary pad research \[ [195](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR195 "Chen W, Chen S, Morsi Y, El-Hamshary H, El-Newhy M, Fan C, Mo X (2016) Superabsorbent 3D scaffold based on electrospun nanofibers for cartilage tissue engineering. ACS Appl Mater Interfaces 8:24415–24425.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.6b06825                                    ")\].

ACS Appl Mater Interfaces 8:24415–24425.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.6b06825                                    ")\].   Aminyan _et. al._ designed a SAF to create a more feasible and versatile method for mass production of SAFs \[ [196](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR196 "Aminyan R, Bazgir S (2019) Fabrication and characterization of nanofibrous polyacrylic acid superabsorbent using gas-assisted electrospinning technique. React Funct Polym 141:133–144.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2019.05.012                                    ")\]. As summarized in Table [9](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab9), poly(acrylic acid) (PAA) was crosslinked with 1,4-butanediol diglycidyl-ether to yield an electrospun network that absorbs 90 000% and 30 000% for distilled water and saline solution, respectively. Absorption under load test showed that the SAF had an absorption capacity of 25 000% in saline solution \[ [196](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR196 "Aminyan R, Bazgir S (2019) Fabrication and characterization of nanofibrous polyacrylic acid superabsorbent using gas-assisted electrospinning technique. React Funct Polym 141:133–144.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2019.05.012                                    ")\]. In a second investigation, Ranjbari _et. al._ made the same SAF with a needless electrospinning technique \[ [197](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR197 "Ranjbari E, Bazgir S, Shirazi MMA (2020) Needleless electrospinning of poly(acrylic acid) superabsorbent: fabrication, characterization and swelling behavior. Polym Test 84:106403.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.polymertesting.2020.106403                                    ")\]. The absorption changed to 51 000% and 17 000% in distilled water and saline solution, respectively. These results showcase the difference the spun technique has on absorption. Figure [25](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig25) shows the difference needless electrospinning makes in fibre size when electrospinning SAFs \[ [197](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR197 "Ranjbari E, Bazgir S, Shirazi MMA (2020) Needleless electrospinning of poly(acrylic acid) superabsorbent: fabrication, characterization and swelling behavior. Polym Test 84:106403.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.polymertesting.2020.106403                                    ")\].
