React Funct Polym 141:133–144.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2019.05.012                                    ")\]. Reprinted from React Funct Polym, 141, Aminyan R, Bazgir S, Fabrication and characterization of nanofibrous polyacrylic acid superabsorbent using gas-assisted electrospinning technique, Copyright (2019), with permission from Elsevier.
  
  Wei _et. al._ designed an absorbing core for use in absorbing hygiene products with wearer comfort and health in mind. A core that absorbs quickly, quicker than the commercially used cellulose pulp mixed with SAP spheres, will eliminate a constant ‘wet’ feeling and potential skin issues and bacterial growth. Therefore, they designed a three layer micro/nano superabsorbent fibre core, prepared with solution blow spinning a solution of micron-sized hydrolysed polyacrylonitrile and a nano-sized SAF. The SAF content in each layer progressively increases to decrease the pore size from 30 µm to 16 µm and then 7 µm, increasing wettability. The results were promising, showing rapid infiltration capability, reduced reverse osmosis, and increased absorption and retention rates compared to commercial diapers \[ [198](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR198 "Wei D, Yu B, Chen D, Fan G, Yan W, Cui W, Zhang Q, Chen Y, Xiong Y, Qin X, Wang R, Jin X, He J (2024) Micro-nanofiber three-dimensional antibacterial sponge with wetting/pore dual gradient for rapid liquid infiltration and uniform retention in diapers. ACS Appl Mater Interfaces.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.4c15094                                    ")\].
  
  The SAFs mentioned here show better absorbing capacities than the SAPs in the previous section. From the SEM images, it is clear that surface to volume ratio and the pore size between fibres are contributing factors. Additionally, the preparation methods, electrospinning and solution blowing, are scalable and adoptable in industry.
  
  #### Current work on green crosslinkers
  
  Majority of SAPs on the market today are of synthetic origin, made from acrylate and acrylamide-based monomers, polymerized in the presence of crosslinking agents by radical polymerization. The nature of these crosslinking agents is an important factor to consider when trying to reduce the ecological impact of the SAP.
  
  They should also be biocompatible, biodegradable, and non-toxic, while having high water absorption and retention capacities. Therefore, natural alternatives that use crosslinking methods such as acetalization are being investigated \[ [201](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR201 "Bachra Y, Grouli A, Damiri F, Talbi M, Berrada M (2021) A novel superabsorbent polymer from crosslinked carboxymethyl tragacanth gum with glutaraldehyde: synthesis, characterization, and swelling properties. Int J Biomater 2021:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2021/5008833                                    ")\]. Dialdehydes \[ [202](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR202 "Ravishankar K, Dhamodharan R (2020) Advances in chitosan-based hydrogels: evolution from covalently crosslinked systems to ionotropically crosslinked superabsorbents. React Funct Polym 149:104517.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2020.104517                                    ")\], citric acid \[ [203](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR203 "Prasad C, Park SY, Lee JS, Park JJ, Jang Y, Lee SW, Lee B-M, Nam Y-R, Rao AK, Choi HY (2023) Modeling and investigation of swelling kinetics of sodium carboxymethyl cellulose/starch/citric acid superabsorbent polymer. Int J Biol Macromol 253:127013.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.127013                                    "), [204](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR204 "Chen J, Chan DY, Yang T, Parisi D, Reuvers B, Veldhuis T, Picchioni F, Wu J, Raffa P, Koning C (2025) Bio-degradable, fully bio-based, thermally cross-linked superabsorbent polymers from citric acid and glycerol. Green Chem 27:3234–3247.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D4GC06323F                                    ")\], genipin \[ [202](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR202 "Ravishankar K, Dhamodharan R (2020) Advances in chitosan-based hydrogels: evolution from covalently crosslinked systems to ionotropically crosslinked superabsorbents. React Funct Polym 149:104517.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2020.104517                                    ")\], tartaric acid \[ [205](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR205 "Tan T, Zhou J, Gao X, Tang X, Zhang H (2021) Synthesis, characterization and water-absorption behavior of tartaric acid-modified cellulose gel fromcorn stalk pith. Ind Crops Prod 169:113641.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2021.113641                                    ")\], succinic acid \[ [206](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR206 "Kwon YR, Kim HC, Kim JS, Chang Y-W, Kim DH (2023) Surface-crosslinking of itaconic acid-based superabsorbent polymer using a novel bio-based surface-crosslinker based on succinic acid. Polym-Plast Technol Mater 62:1057–1066.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/25740881.2023.2189450                                    ")\], lignans \[ [207](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR207 "Manu KD, Gupta RK (2024) Natural polymers-humic acid and lignin based hydrogels: in agriculture, environment and energy storage. Ind Crops Prod 219:119029.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2024.119029                                    "), [208](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR208 "Susmanto P, Putri AJ, Parwati A (2024) Synthesis of sustainable superabsorbent biopolymer: modified rice straw cellulose with initiator and crosslink agent. Ecol Chem Eng S 31:527–538.                    https://doi.org/10.2478/eces-2024-0034                                    ")\], and tannins \[ [209](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR209 "Bidarvandi N, Sabzevari A, Kabiri K, Ghasemzadeh H, Moini N (2024) Preparation of green-stretchable hydrogel based on succinic acid-based monomer with antibacterial properties. Polym-Plast Technol Mater 63:1769–1779.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/25740881.2024.2358998                                    "), [210](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR210 "Xu J, Li Y, Chen Y, Wang L, Liao M (2021) Preparation and characterization of a novel polysialic acid/gelatin composite hydrogels cross-linked by tannic acid to improve wound healing after cesarean section dressing. J Biomater Sci Polym Ed 32:1927–1943.                    https://doi.org/10.1080/09205063.2021.1950961                                    "), [211](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR211 "Sahiner M, Demirci S, Sahiner N (2024) Super porous carboxymethyl cellulose-tannic acid (TA@CMC) cryogels with antioxidant, antibacterial, and α-glucosidase enzyme inhibition abilities. Polysaccharides 5:823–841.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/polysaccharides5040051                                    "), [212](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR212 "Karakuş NR, Türk S, Guney Eskiler G, Syzdykbayev M, Appazov NO, Özacar M (2024) Investigation of tannic acid crosslinked PVA/PEI-based hydrogels as potential wound dressings with self-healing and high antibacterial properties. Gels 10:682.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/gels10110682                                    "), [213](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR213 "Parvathy PA, Ayisha Ristha TP, De S, Lakshmanan V, Sahoo SK (2025) Tannic acid-grafted P(NIPAm- co -BioAcc) copolymeric flexible hydrogels with tunable functional properties. ACS Appl Eng Mater 3:465–481.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsaenm.4c00784                                    ")\] are among these natural alternatives.
  
  Glutaraldehyde is a commonly used dialdehyde for crosslinking chitosan \[ [202](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR202 "Ravishankar K, Dhamodharan R (2020) Advances in chitosan-based hydrogels: evolution from covalently crosslinked systems to ionotropically crosslinked superabsorbents. React Funct Polym 149:104517.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.reactfunctpolym.2020.104517                                    ")\] and gums \[ [201](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR201 "Bachra Y, Grouli A, Damiri F, Talbi M, Berrada M (2021) A novel superabsorbent polymer from crosslinked carboxymethyl tragacanth gum with glutaraldehyde: synthesis, characterization, and swelling properties. Int J Biomater 2021:1–14.                    https://doi.org/10.1155/2021/5008833                                    ")\] as its relatively inexpensive and reacts quickly with the free non-protonated amino groups in biomacromolecules. The aldehyde groups form imine bonds without harsh reaction conditions or extra chemicals needed to stimulate the reaction. Glutaraldehyde shows good porosity \[ [214](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR214 "Doustdar F, Olad A, Ghorbani M (2022) Effect of glutaraldehyde and calcium chloride as different crosslinking agents on the characteristics of chitosan/cellulose nanocrystals scaffold. Int J Biol Macromol 208:912–924.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2022.03.193                                    ")\] and good reswelling ability \[ [215](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR215 "Kaçoğlu HS, Ceylan Ö, Çelebi M (2024) Swelling and re-swelling performance of glutaraldehyde crosslinked wet chitosan hydrogels. J Innov Eng Nat Sci 5:29–42.                    https://doi.org/10.61112/jiens.1484566                                    ")\] when used in chitosan hydrogels. However, Glutaraldehyde has a notable cytotoxicity that limits its biocompatibility \[ [216](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR216 "Wang W, Huang W-C, Zheng J, Xue C, Mao X (2023) Preparation and comparison of dialdehyde derivatives of polysaccharides as cross-linking agents. Int J Biol Macromol 236:123913.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.123913                                    ")\].
  
  By oxidizing polysaccharide polymers (see Fig. [26](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Fig26)), dialdehyde derivatives of the polysaccharides are formed that can be used as a better biocompatible alternative while enhancing rheological, structural strength, and gelation properties of the 3D scaffold. Wang _et. al._ created dialdehyde derivatives of several polysaccharides (corn starch, potato starch, alginate, pectin, and glucan) and used it to crosslink chitosan polymers. The tests showed that the derivatives had comparable crosslinking and gelation properties to glutaraldehyde and genipin, while having better biocompatibility \[ [216](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR216 "Wang W, Huang W-C, Zheng J, Xue C, Mao X (2023) Preparation and comparison of dialdehyde derivatives of polysaccharides as cross-linking agents. Int J Biol Macromol 236:123913.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.123913                                    ")\].
  
  **Figure 26**
  
  ![Figure 26](https://media.springernature.com/lw685/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1007%2Fs10853-025-11151-7/MediaObjects/10853_2025_11151_Fig26_HTML.png)The alternative text for this image may have been generated using AI.
  
  [Full size image](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/figures/26)
  
  Dialdehyde formation scheme.
  
  Non-favourable mechanical properties of polysaccharide-based SAPs are issues that limits its widespread use. Therefore, Qiu _et. al._ attempted to design a dialdehyde-cellulose-nanofiber-based hydrogel by oxidizing cellulose nanofibers with periodate in a suspension and freeze-drying the suspension to obtain the aerogel. No organic solvent and no crosslinking agent were used, as the dialdehyde groups crosslinked with hydroxyl groups on its neighbouring cellulose nanofibers. This gel showed good flexibility, resistance to stress, and modification ability for a variety of applications \[ [217](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR217 "Qiu X, Wang S, Chen S (2022) The self-assembly of dialdehyde-cellulose-nanofiber-based hydrogels with high compression resilience. Cellulose 29:5645–5658.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s10570-022-04605-7                                    ")\]. Another aerogel with good compression resilience was designed with dialdehyde carboxymethyl cellulose as crosslinker by Zhang _et. al._ \[ [218](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR218 "Zhang F, Wang C, Mu C, Lin W (2022) A novel hydrophobic all-biomass aerogel reinforced by dialdehyde carboxymethyl cellulose for oil/organic solvent-water separation. Polymer (Guildf) 238:124402.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.polymer.2021.124402                                    ")\]. The low toxicity of oxidized polysaccharides can attribute to steric hindrance by the long chains and size of the polymers, as well as the fact that the polymers prevent the oxidized portions from being transported into cells. Based on this, Jiang _et. al_ designed a chitosan SAP crosslinked with dialdehyde carboxymethyl cellulose and tested its biocompatibility and swelling capacity compared to the chitosan SAP crosslinked with glutaraldehyde. The cellulose crosslinked SAP showed to be non-toxic, biodegradable, to have better thermal stability, swelling capacity, and cytocompatibility \[ [219](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR219 "Jiang X, Yang Z, Peng Y, Han B, Li Z, Li X, Liu W (2016) Preparation, characterization and feasibility study of dialdehyde carboxymethyl cellulose as a novel crosslinking reagent. Carbohydr Polym 137:632–641.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.carbpol.2015.10.078                                    ")\].
  
  Amine-terminated polyethylene glycol polymers were crosslinked with dialdehyde potato starch with Schiff-base crosslinking. The resulting hydrogel showed remarkable water absorption capacity of 4440%, after soaking for 24 h \[ [220](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR220 "Zhang W, Sun XL, Yang Q, Guo Y, Cui Y, Xiang Y, Hu B, Wei J, Tu P (2024) In situ forming of PEG-NH2/dialdehyde starch Schiff-base hydrogels and their application in slow-release urea. Int J Biol Macromol 256:128355.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.128355                                    ")\]. A dialdehyde cassava starch with dynamic crosslinking with gelatin was designed by Xu _et. al._ \[ [221](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR221 "Xu Z, Ye M, Li L, Dong Z, Su X, Zhao J, Li J (2025) Recyclable high-performance and fully biodegradable cassava starch composite hydrogels based on dynamic cross-linking. Int J Biol Macromol 310:143117.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2025.143117                                    ")\]. Prepared by simply oxidizing cassava starch with sodium periodate (NaIO4), and mixing it into a gelatin mixture. The hydrogel made showed tensile strength (133.22 kPa) and compressive strength (1035.9 kPa), with swelling capacity of ~ 3 g/g after being immersed for 300 h in a phosphate buffer solution, and self-healing properties. A soil burial test showed that it undergoes 100% degradation within 22 days in neutral soil and 23 days in alkaline soil, with degradation being accelerated in acidic soil (17 days) \[ [221](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR221 "Xu Z, Ye M, Li L, Dong Z, Su X, Zhao J, Li J (2025) Recyclable high-performance and fully biodegradable cassava starch composite hydrogels based on dynamic cross-linking. Int J Biol Macromol 310:143117.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2025.143117                                    ")\].
  
  Citric acid is a popular crosslinker due to its biodegradability \[ [222](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR222 "Maske VA, Jangale S, Vyavahare S, More AP (2025) Development of coconut shell charcoal-reinforced starch superabsorbent composites, with the comparative study of epichlorohydrin and citric acid as the crosslinking agents. Starch - Stärke.                    https://doi.org/10.1002/star.202300240                                    ")\], improving mechanical properties \[ [223](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR223 "Liu K, Dong X, Wang Y, Wu X, Dai H (2022) Dopamine-modified chitosan hydrogel for spinal cord injury. Carbohydr Polym 298:120047.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.carbpol.2022.120047                                    ")\], affordability, and mild reaction conditions \[ [203](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR203 "Prasad C, Park SY, Lee JS, Park JJ, Jang Y, Lee SW, Lee B-M, Nam Y-R, Rao AK, Choi HY (2023) Modeling and investigation of swelling kinetics of sodium carboxymethyl cellulose/starch/citric acid superabsorbent polymer. Int J Biol Macromol 253:127013.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.127013                                    ")\]. Prasad _et. al._ designed a CMC and starch SAP crosslinked with citric acid via esterification. They made SAPs with different citric acid ratios to compare the effect of crosslinker ratios. Results show that water absorbance capacity and reswelling is best for the SAP with the smallest polymer to crosslinker ratio, being ~ 280 g/g, and retaining > 80% of initial water absorbency after three reswelling \[ [203](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR203 "Prasad C, Park SY, Lee JS, Park JJ, Jang Y, Lee SW, Lee B-M, Nam Y-R, Rao AK, Choi HY (2023) Modeling and investigation of swelling kinetics of sodium carboxymethyl cellulose/starch/citric acid superabsorbent polymer. Int J Biol Macromol 253:127013.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2023.127013                                    ")\]. A fully bio-based SAP, with self-linking citric acid-based polymers was designed by Chen _et. al._ Citric acid and glycerol was used as initiating monomers to produce polymers that undergo self-crosslinking. Here, temperature determined the density of crosslinking. The optimal crosslinking temperature was 140 °C, having water absorption capacity of 24 g/g and a biodegradability of > 60% after 28 days \[ [204](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR204 "Chen J, Chan DY, Yang T, Parisi D, Reuvers B, Veldhuis T, Picchioni F, Wu J, Raffa P, Koning C (2025) Bio-degradable, fully bio-based, thermally cross-linked superabsorbent polymers from citric acid and glycerol. Green Chem 27:3234–3247.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D4GC06323F                                    ")\].
  
  Genipin is derived from the fruits of _Genipa americana_ and _Gardenia jasminoides_. It can easily crosslink with polysaccharides, is biocompatible \[ [224](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR224 "Wahba MI (2024) A comprehensive review on genipin: an efficient natural cross-linker for biopolymers. Polym Bull 81:14251–14305.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s00289-024-05406-7                                    ")\], and improves physical and mechanical properties of bio-based materials \[ [225](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR225 "Hurtado Colmenares LB, Nejati M, Fang Y, Guo B, Jiménez-Quero A, Capezza AJ, Sabino MA (2024) New sources of genipin-rich substances for crosslinking future manufactured bio-based materials. RSC Sustain 2:125–138.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D3SU00303E                                    ")\], Crosslinking with primary amines on polymer chains can occur with two possible mechanisms. One being a nucleophilic attach on the β-carbon of the C = C moiety in the cyclic structure, and the other being a Schiff base reaction with the aldehyde moiety. Concentration, pH, and temperature has an influence on mechanism of crosslinking and degree of crosslinking \[ [224](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR224 "Wahba MI (2024) A comprehensive review on genipin: an efficient natural cross-linker for biopolymers. Polym Bull 81:14251–14305.                    https://doi.org/10.1007/s00289-024-05406-7                                    ")\]. Hurtado _et. al._ designed a wheat gluten SAP with genipap oil (containing 55.4% genipin \[ [225](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR225 "Hurtado Colmenares LB, Nejati M, Fang Y, Guo B, Jiménez-Quero A, Capezza AJ, Sabino MA (2024) New sources of genipin-rich substances for crosslinking future manufactured bio-based materials. RSC Sustain 2:125–138.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D3SU00303E                                    ")\]) as crosslinker, through reactive extrusion. The maximum water absorption capacity achieved was 471%, by a pure genipin crosslinked SAP, and ~ 350% by a genipap oil crosslinked SAP. However, since genipin stabilizes a polymer 3D structure, biodegradation took longer (23–43 days) than the control SAP (14–35 days) \[ [226](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR226 "Hurtado LB, Jiménez-Rosado M, Nejati M, Rasheed F, Prade T, Jiménez-Quero A, Sabino MA, Capezza AJ (2024) Genipap oil as a natural cross-linker for biodegradable and low-ecotoxicity porous absorbents via reactive extrusion. Biomacromol 25:7642–7659.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.biomac.4c00883                                    ")\]. A chitosan and gelatin-based SAP was crosslinked with genipin by Moshayedi _et. al._ to create a highly porous SAP \[ [227](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR227 "Moshayedi S, Sarpoolaky H, Khavandi A (2021) Fabrication, swelling behavior, and water absorption kinetics of genipin-crosslinked gelatin–chitosan hydrogels. Polym Eng Sci 61:3094–3103.                    https://doi.org/10.1002/pen.25821                                    ")\]. They found that increasing genipin concentration in the SAP mixture, decreases the SAPs pore size. The water absorption capacity of the SAP higher concentrations of genipin were lower (1000%—1100%). Water absorption capacities were between 1200 and 1300% for those with lower genipin concentrations. Further tests revealed that the hydrogels possess hydrolytic resistance and biocompatibility \[ [227](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR227 "Moshayedi S, Sarpoolaky H, Khavandi A (2021) Fabrication, swelling behavior, and water absorption kinetics of genipin-crosslinked gelatin–chitosan hydrogels. Polym Eng Sci 61:3094–3103.                    https://doi.org/10.1002/pen.25821                                    ")\].
  
  Tan _et. al._ crosslinked modified corn stalk pith cellulose with tartaric acid with an esterification reaction \[ [205](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR205 "Tan T, Zhou J, Gao X, Tang X, Zhang H (2021) Synthesis, characterization and water-absorption behavior of tartaric acid-modified cellulose gel fromcorn stalk pith. Ind Crops Prod 169:113641.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2021.113641                                    ")\]. The SAP produced was ultralight (1.894 mg/cm3) and showed good water absorption capacity of 417 g/g. SEM revealed that tartaric acid crosslinked cellulose SAP produced a highly porous SAP \[ [205](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR205 "Tan T, Zhou J, Gao X, Tang X, Zhang H (2021) Synthesis, characterization and water-absorption behavior of tartaric acid-modified cellulose gel fromcorn stalk pith. Ind Crops Prod 169:113641.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.indcrop.2021.113641                                    ")\].
  
  Vanillin (4-hydroxy-3-methoxybenzaldehyde) is a natural molecule with an aldehyde group that finds many applications in the perfume, pharmaceutical, beverage, and food industry. Now, it is an attractive option for crosslinking polymers to form SAPs. Its low molecular weight allows it to easily penetrate the long chains of polymers and interact with its amine and/or hydroxyl groups with its aldehyde group forming imine and/or acetyl links. The loose end of the vanillin molecule has a hydroxyl group forming strong hydrogen bonds with hydroxyl groups in polysaccharides \[ [166](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR166 "Czarnecka E, Nowaczyk J, Prochoń M, Masek A (2022) Nanoarchitectonics for biodegradable superabsorbent based on carboxymethyl starch and chitosan cross-linked with vanillin. Int J Mol Sci 23:5386.                    https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms23105386                                    ")\].
  
  ### Current work on biodegradable plastics
  
  Green chemistry advocates for the use of eco-friendly raw materials, solvents, and reagents. It tries to minimize the use and production of harmful substances, and facilitates resource and waste material recycling \[ [228](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR228 "Deng J, Zhu E-Q, Xu G-F, Naik N, Murugadoss V, Ma M-G, Guo Z, Shi Z-J (2022) Overview of renewable polysaccharide-based composites for biodegradable food packaging applications. Green Chem 24:480–492.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D1GC03898B                                    ")\]. Plastics used in single use plastic items have disposal and recycling challenges according to green chemistry. The properties that make them so useful, antibacterial, light blocking, gas barrier, and anti-oxidant effects, are the very properties that create these environmental issues. Some polysaccharides have the potential to provide these characteristics as replacements for petrochemical plastics, with the added benefit of being biodegradable and renewable \[ [228](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR228 "Deng J, Zhu E-Q, Xu G-F, Naik N, Murugadoss V, Ma M-G, Guo Z, Shi Z-J (2022) Overview of renewable polysaccharide-based composites for biodegradable food packaging applications. Green Chem 24:480–492.                    https://doi.org/10.1039/D1GC03898B                                    ")\].
  
  Depending on what function the plastic has in an end product, it needs to have certain properties. Table [10](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab10) displays some of the properties that make polyethylene and polypropylene the plastic of choice for the manufacturing of products \[ [229](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR229 "Farahmand F, Shokrollahi P, Mehrabzadeh M (2003) Recycling of commingled plastics wastecontaining polyvinylchloride, polypropylene,polyethylene and paper. Iran Polym J 12:185–190")\].
  
  **Table 10 The mechanical properties of polyethylene and polypropylene**
  
  [Full size table](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/tables/10)
  
  Table [11](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab11) summarizes the recent work done on biodegradable polymers for biodegradable films and plastics. Starch is one of the most studied polymers due to its abundant availability, low cost, and biodegradability. However, plastics made form starch are brittle and have inferior mechanical properties to petroleum-based plastics. Therefore, it is are blended with other biopolymers to improve its inferior mechanical properties. Chitosan, for example, is added to polyvinyl alcohol as it increases tensile strength, due to its good film-forming properties, biodegradability, crystallinity, and other mechanical properties. Glycerol improves physical properties, mechanical properties, and protects plastics from microorganisms. Ayyubi _et. al._ designed a biodegradable composite from cassava starch, chitosan, PVA, and glycerol as plasticiser \[ [230](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR230 "Ayyubi SN, Purbasari A, Kusmiyati (2022) The effect of composition on mechanical properties of biodegradable plastic based on chitosan/cassava starch/PVA/crude glycerol: optimization of the composition using Box Behnken design. Mater Today Proc 63:78–83.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matpr.2022.01.294                                    ")\]. After the film has dried for 1 week, the mechanical properties of the film were analysed. The tensile strength (25.11 MPa) and elongation at break (83.43%) of the film is attributed to intermolecular hydrogen bonding between the NH3+ on the chitosan and the OH− from starch. Its biodegradability after 30 days is 50.45% \[ [230](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR230 "Ayyubi SN, Purbasari A, Kusmiyati (2022) The effect of composition on mechanical properties of biodegradable plastic based on chitosan/cassava starch/PVA/crude glycerol: optimization of the composition using Box Behnken design. Mater Today Proc 63:78–83.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.matpr.2022.01.294                                    ")\]. Starch from other sources such as purple yam \[ [231](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR231 "Martins da Costa JC, Lima Miki KS, da Silva RA, Teixeira-Costa BE (2020) Development of biodegradable films based on purple yam starch/chitosan for food application. Heliyon 6:e03718.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.heliyon.2020.e03718                                    ")\], water chestnut \[ [232](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR232 "Dularia C, Sinhmar A, Thory R, Pathera AK, Nain V (2019) Development of starch nanoparticles based composite films from non-conventional source - water chestnut (Trapa bispinosa). Int J Biol Macromol 136:1161–1168.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2019.06.169                                    ")\], khitul \[ [233](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR233 "Sudheesh C, Sunooj KV, Sasidharan A, Sabu S, Basheer A, Navaf M, Raghavender C, Sinha SK, George J (2020) Energetic neutral N2 atoms treatment on the kithul (Caryota urens) starch biodegradable film: physico-chemical characterization. Food Hydrocoll 103:105650.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.foodhyd.2020.105650                                    ")\], turmeric \[ [234](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR234 "Oluba OM, Osayame E, Shoyombo AO (2021) Production and characterization of keratin-starch bio-composite film from chicken feather waste and turmeric starch. Biocatal Agric Biotechnol 33:101996.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bcab.2021.101996                                    ")\], and sugar palm \[ [235](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR235 "Hasan M, Rusman R, Khaldun I, Ardana L, Mudatsir M, Fansuri H (2020) Active edible sugar palm starch-chitosan films carrying extra virgin olive oil: Barrier, thermo-mechanical, antioxidant, and antimicrobial properties. Int J Biol Macromol 163:766–775.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2020.07.076                                    ")\] have also been copolymerised to form plastics (see Table [11](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab11)).
  
  **Table 11 Biodegradable polymers used in plastic research**
  
  [Full size table](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7/tables/11)
  
  A seaweed-based plastic was created by Hanry _et. al._ with kappaphycus alvarezii polysaccharide and glycerol as copolymer \[ [241](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR241 "Hanry EL, Surugau N (2023) Optimization of biomass-to-water ratio and glycerol content to develop bioplastics from whole seaweed, Kappaphycus alvarezii.                    https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-2757261/v1                                    ")\]. They tested the plastic’s water permeability (3.85–1.32 × 10–15 g/m.s.Pa), biodegradability (14.3 days), the tensile strength (7.2 MPa), and elongation at break (14.7%) \[ [241](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR241 "Hanry EL, Surugau N (2023) Optimization of biomass-to-water ratio and glycerol content to develop bioplastics from whole seaweed, Kappaphycus alvarezii.                    https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-2757261/v1                                    ")\]. Kartika _et. al._ created a film with cellulose, PVA, and chitosan \[ [242](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR242 "Kartika EY, Saepudin E (2020) Synthesis of crosslinked cellulose/PVA bioplastic strengthened with chitosan as and alternative to conventional plastics. In: Proceedings of the 2nd International Conference on Science, Technology, and Environment. SCITEPRESS - Science and Technology Publications, pp 17–24")\]. The film showed degradation in ~ 60 days, a tensile strength of 8 MPa, elongation at break of ~ 72%, and water solubility of 67% \[ [242](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR242 "Kartika EY, Saepudin E (2020) Synthesis of crosslinked cellulose/PVA bioplastic strengthened with chitosan as and alternative to conventional plastics. In: Proceedings of the 2nd International Conference on Science, Technology, and Environment. SCITEPRESS - Science and Technology Publications, pp 17–24")\]. A high-performance chitin film was designed by Zhou _et. al._ by crosslinking chitin polymers with epichlorohydrin \[ [243](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR243 "Zhou Y, He Y, Lin X, Feng Y, Liu M (2022) Sustainable, high-performance, and biodegradable plastics made from chitin. ACS Appl Mater Interfaces 14:46980–46993.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.2c12764                                    ")\]. The results showed that the film possessed a water vapour permeability of ~ 4.9 g/m.s.Pa, a degradation rate of ~ 49 days, tensile strength of 104 MPa, and elongation at break of 11% \[ [243](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR243 "Zhou Y, He Y, Lin X, Feng Y, Liu M (2022) Sustainable, high-performance, and biodegradable plastics made from chitin. ACS Appl Mater Interfaces 14:46980–46993.                    https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.2c12764                                    ")\]. Polylactic acid is also a popular polymer due to its favourable properties, having been combined with various copolymers such as poly(ɛ-caprolactone) \[ [237](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR237 "Olewnik-Kruszkowska E, Burkowska-But A, Tarach I, Walczak M, Jakubowska E (2020) Biodegradation of polylactide-based composites with an addition of a compatibilizing agent in different environments. Int Biodeterior Biodegradation 147:104840.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ibiod.2019.104840                                    ")\], polyethylene glycol \[ [238](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR238 "Sharma S, Majumdar A, Butola BS (2021) Tailoring the biodegradability of polylactic acid (PLA) based films and ramie- PLA green composites by using selective additives. Int J Biol Macromol 181:1092–1103.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2021.04.108                                    ")\], polymethylmethacrylate \[ [239](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR239 "Wan L, Zhou S, Zhang Y (2019) Parallel advances in improving mechanical properties and accelerating degradation to polylactic acid. Int J Biol Macromol 125:1093–1102.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijbiomac.2018.12.148                                    ")\], and poly(ethylene glycol) \[ [240](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR240 "Zhang P, He Y, Gao D, Cai Y, Liu B (2019) Hydrolytic and thermal degradation of polyethylene glycol compatibilized poly(lactic acid)-nanocrystalline cellulose bionanocomposites. J Appl Polym Sci 136:46933.                    https://doi.org/10.1002/app.46933                                    ")\]. The majority of the plastics listed in Table [11](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#Tab11) have similar or superior tensile strength or elongation at break compared to polyethylene and polypropylene.
  
  The demand for biodegradable plastics and technological innovation is ever increasing in the packaging industry; therefore, standards and certification for bioplastics have been created in some of its largest producing countries. The three internationally acceptable standardization bodies are the International Organization for Standardization (ISO), the American Society for Testing and Material (ASTM) and the European Committee for Standardization (ECN). The ECN outlines criteria for the bio-based conduct determination of bioplastics, specifying that it should undergo carbon content analysis, elemental analysis, and renewable content analysis. The rate of degradation of biodegradable packaging materials is specified to be 90% degradation within 12 weeks, and complete degradation within 6 months. Additionally, it should be tested for ecotoxicity and heavy metal content. Certifications are given to biodegradable plastics by organizations such as DIN CERTO and Vincotte (European), Biodegradable products Institute (BPI, US), and the Japan bioplastic Association (JBPA, Japan) \[ [244](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR244 "Jayakumar A, Radoor S, Siengchin S, Shin GH, Kim JT (2023) Recent progress of bioplastics in their properties, standards, certifications and regulations: a review. Sci Total Environ 878:163156.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.scitotenv.2023.163156                                    ")\].
  
  The current research seems to be on the right track in terms of meeting the mechanical and physical properties of petroleum-based plastics with the added benefit of biodegradation within weeks. However, crucial data, such as the aforementioned ECN criteria and what the replacement of PP and PE with biopolymers means for the end of life of the products, are not always addressed in these studies.
  
  Disposal options for biodegradable polymers include incineration, anaerobic digestion, home and industrial composting, recycling, and landfilling. When biopolymers degrade, gases are emitted, heat is released, and the nutrients are left in the environment. Gases and heat can be captured and used for power generation. Degraded polymer byproducts/nutrients can be beneficial in fertilizers and incineration can be used to produced energy. Some biopolymers are recyclable; however, it can only be recycled a limited number of times, the sorting and cleaning process my make it impractical, and the polymer should undergo the recycling process before degradation has been triggered \[ [245](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR245 "Yates MR, Barlow CY (2013) Life cycle assessments of biodegradable, commercial biopolymers—a critical review. Resour Conserv Recycl 78:54–66.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.resconrec.2013.06.010                                    ")\].
  
  PLA, for instance, undergoes anaerobic digestion, thus it shows promising end-of-life usage in composting. However, this scenario depends on sorting and cleaning facilities, as PLA does not degrade effectively when mixed with non-compostable items, and high temperature plants, and gas capturing plants. Since these facilities are not readily available, at this stage, PLA is often being landfilled (where methane gas is emitted \[ [246](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR246 "Hottle TA, Bilec MM, Landis AE (2017) Biopolymer production and end of life comparisons using life cycle assessment. Resour Conserv Recycl 122:295–306.                    https://doi.org/10.1016/j.resconrec.2017.03.002                                    ")\] or incinerated with conventional plastics \[ [247](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR247 "Pereira BAM, Martins NO, Dantas SC, Medeiros de Lima A (2025) Evaluation of the substitution of polyethylene for polylactic acid in sanitary pads through life cycle assessment. Sustain Sci Technol 2:024002.                    https://doi.org/10.1088/2977-3504/adbdd2                                    ")\]. Theoretically, it defies the purpose of using the biopolymer. Currently, there is a big gap between laboratory biodegradation/composting studies of biopolymers and municipal waste management systems. Therefore, disposal options are an important factor to consider when assessing end-of-life impact of a product \[ [247](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR247 "Pereira BAM, Martins NO, Dantas SC, Medeiros de Lima A (2025) Evaluation of the substitution of polyethylene for polylactic acid in sanitary pads through life cycle assessment. Sustain Sci Technol 2:024002.                    https://doi.org/10.1088/2977-3504/adbdd2                                    ")\].
  
  In South Africa, landfilling is the primary method of waste management. Many landfill sites are above ground, where waste is exposed to the elements, burning, and can get blown away by wind, scattering it across the environment \[ [78](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10853-025-11151-7#ref-CR78 "Mazhandu ZS, Muzenda E, Belaid M, Mamvura TA, Nhubu T (2021) A review of plastic waste management practices what can South Africa learn? Adv Sci Technol Eng Syst J 6:1013–1028.                    https://doi.org/10.25046/aj0602116                                    ")\]. In this case, composting, incineration, anaerobic digestion, and recycling may not be feasible options when designing biopolymers with promising end-of-life usage in mind. Degradation via sun (UV, IR) exposure, rain (hydrolysis) exposure, insect/soil exposure, air (oxidation) exposure, or burning should rather be considered.
  
  ## Conclusion and the future
  
  Disposable sanitary products represent a significant global challenge within the broader context of health and environmental management. Menstrual products, as a key example, often fail to support a dignified and informed menstrual experience for many individuals. Educational programs and menstrual health management (MHM) activities frequently overlook critical aspects such as the composition, production, disposal, and broader impacts of these products—on individuals, communities, and the environment.
  
  While disposable sanitary pads have become a widely used solution, they bring numerous environmental, health, economic, and social concerns. Encouragingly, research is increasingly focused on developing sustainable alternatives, including the use of biopolymers to replace conventional polypropylene (PP), polyethylene (PE), and superabsorbent polymers (SAPs). This review highlights the promise of such materials, though further investigation is needed into the scalability, industrial production methods, and life-cycle impacts of biopolymer-based nonwovens and SAPs.
  
  It is also essential to shift away from outdated, patriarchal narratives that stigmatize menstruation, and instead advocate for menstrual health and hygiene practices that are safe, sustainable, affordable, and accessible. Through awareness, education, and innovation, we can reimagine menstrual care in a way that no longer compromises health, dignity, or the environment.
